Some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. Is that helpful to tell you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so. I might feel a little silly if I’ve been picking at sweets instead of making a proper meal, but I wouldn’t call it guilt exactly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Not like a real binge, I don’t think. I mean… sometimes I’ll stand at the counter and keep eating crackers or sweets, and then realize I’ve had more than I meant to. But it’s not exactly a big meal or anything. It’s more that my eating’s gotten sort of mixed up—some days I forget lunch, and other days I pick at things even though they don’t really taste right. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s always stress, exactly. Sometimes I just sort of realize I’ve skipped lunch, and other times I’m standing there eating crackers or something sweet even though it doesn’t taste very good. I suppose being upset might make it easier to do that, but mostly it just feels like my eating is all turned around. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood has anything to do with when you eat those crackers or sweets?\n\nPatient: I think it might, yes… at least a little. What really made me come in is that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. I don’t know if that’s exactly mood, but it doesn’t feel like my normal self. Is that the kind of connection you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers or sweets helps you feel better when you're upset?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but not in a real way. It’s not like I eat them and suddenly feel better. It’s more that my eating has just gotten all turned around… some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’ll be at the counter eating crackers or sweets even though they don’t taste right. I don’t know if that counts as trying to feel better, but it doesn’t feel like my usual pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat those crackers or sweets in an attempt to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it on purpose to improve my mood, exactly. It’s more… what made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets even though nothing tastes right. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating when you're not really hungry is a way for you to deal with feeling upset or stressed?\n\nPatient: I suppose it could be, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I don’t always feel hungry when I’m doing it. Some days I just forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets at the counter, even though they don’t really taste right. I’m not sure if that’s me dealing with being upset or stressed, but my eating is definitely not like it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is a way for you to cope with your emotions?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I’m not sure I’d call it coping exactly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing something dramatic. It’s just that what made me come in is my eating feels all turned around lately. Some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’ll find myself standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. I guess it could be tied to my emotions, but I’m not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits have changed as a way to manage or regulate your emotions?\n\nPatient: I guess it might be, but I don’t really know that I’m doing it to manage my feelings on purpose. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets even though nothing tastes right. What made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around—some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. I suppose being upset might make it easier to do that, but mostly it just feels like my eating is all turned around. What really made me come in is that my eating’s gotten all turned around. I’m not sure if that’s me dealing with being upset or stressed, but my eating is definitely not like it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is a way for you to cope with your emotions?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I’m not sure I’d call it coping exactly. I’m not sure if that counts as being less interested in food.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve lost a significant amount, no… at least I haven’t noticed it that way. It’s more the eating itself that’s gotten turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. Is that helpful to tell you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so. I might feel a little silly if I’ve been picking at sweets instead of making a proper meal, but I wouldn’t call it guilt exactly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Not like a real binge, I don’t think. I mean… sometimes I’ll stand at the counter and keep eating crackers or sweets, and then realize I’ve had more than I meant to. But it’s not exactly a big meal or anything. It’s more that my eating’s gotten sort of mixed up—some days I forget lunch, and other days I pick at things even though they don’t really taste right. Sometimes I just sort of realize I’ve skipped lunch, and other times I’m standing there eating crackers or something sweet even though it doesn’t taste very good. I suppose being upset might make it easier to do that, but mostly it just feels like my eating is all turned around. What really made me come in is that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. I don’t know if that’s exactly mood, but it doesn’t feel like my normal self. It’s more that my eating has just gotten all turned around… some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’ll be at the counter eating crackers or sweets even though they don’t taste right. I don’t know if that counts as trying to feel better, but it doesn’t feel like my usual pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat those crackers or sweets in an attempt to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it on purpose to improve my mood, exactly. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating when you're not really hungry is a way for you to deal with feeling upset or stressed?\n\nPatient: I suppose it could be, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I don’t always feel hungry when I’m doing it. Some days I just forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets at the counter, even though they don’t really taste right. I’m not sure if that’s me dealing with being upset or stressed, but my eating is definitely not like it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is a way for you to cope with your emotions?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I’m not sure I’d call it coping exactly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing something dramatic. It’s just that what made me come in is my eating feels all turned around lately.
